- **design-completeness**: Writing or reviewing a design doc, PRD, RFC, or technical plan before code, or thinking "this design feels thin but I can't say why": each behavior MUST name three: contract (inputs, outputs, invariants), failure modes (what can go wrong and what happens then), edges (boundary, empty, zero, maximum cases). Happy-path-only is unfinished; a present-but-empty section (TBD, TODO) counts as missing, not done. Not about one term carrying two meanings (that is one-meaning-per-term); this checks every case is named. Reach for it even when it looks complete: the unwritten failure mode is invisible. - **design-completeness**: Before code is written, a design MUST name three things for every behavior it specifies, the three a complete design of its type always has. A design that states only the happy path is not finished, and a section that is present but empty (TBD, TODO, to be detailed) is a gap wearing the costume of completeness.
